Digital Footprint Verification and Contact Intelligence: U.S. Kitchen and Bathroom Remodeling Contractors (Rows 1084–1183)Executive Summary and Digital Channel ArchitectureAn examination of the 100 small kitchen and bathroom remodeling businesses in Part 01 (rows 1084 through 1183) reveals a pronounced structural divide in how local residential trade contractors manage digital visibility and client acquisition. Across the full 100-business cohort, 47 enterprises (rows 1084–1130) maintain an established social media anchor—specifically 37 with a Facebook business page and 10 with an Instagram profile—while 53 businesses (rows 1131–1183) operate exclusively via a Google Business Profile without any pre-linked social media or web domain. This bifurcated landscape stems from the low barrier to entry of mobile-first social platforms compared to the recurring hosting, maintenance, and search-optimization costs required to sustain a dedicated corporate domain.In the deeply audited initial tranche (rows 1084 through 1100), multi-source verification across business registries, Better Business Bureau (BBB) records, trade directories, and social platforms confirms that small remodeling contractors overwhelmingly rely on direct telephone and social messaging channels rather than published email addresses or standalone websites. Even when contractors actively upload project photography—such as custom shower pan installations, luxury vinyl plank flooring retrofits, or exterior siding renovations—their post captions consistently direct prospective clients to call, text, or send a direct message rather than correspond via email. Furthermore, legacy web domains identified in third-party business directories (dreamkitchensplus.com, dream-kitchens-plus.business.site, and clevelandhomeremodeler.com) have lapsed into inactive status as contractors consolidate their marketing footprint onto zero-maintenance social feeds, signaling a broader industry shift toward platform-hosted portfolios.Verification Analysis and Entity Disambiguation (Rows 1084–1100)Cross-referencing state and municipal contractor lists, BBB accreditation profiles, Houzz portfolios, and social media activity across rows 1084 through 1100 illuminates critical operational mechanisms governing small-scale remodeling data hygiene. In local construction and restoration trades, identical business names frequently appear across unrelated jurisdictions due to generic trade naming conventions, creating substantial risk of false-positive contact attribution if geographic and telephonic identifiers are not rigorously cross-checked. Specifically, for Black Cat Restoration (row 1095, located at 2250 Military Rd, Tonawanda, NY 14150, phone 716-602-2620), Western New York directory records confirm its operation as a local kitchen remodeling contractor. Open-web queries for the business name surface an active domain (https://www.blackcatrestoration.com) and direct work email (BlackCatRestoration@Outlook.com) managed by owner Diane Kemp. However, forensic inspection of that website and its corresponding Angi profile proves that it belongs to a furniture repair and reupholstery studio operating within a two-hour radius of Brandon, Florida. Adhering to strict entity verification standards, the Florida domain and email are excluded from row 1095 to prevent cross-entity contamination.A second mechanism affecting contact discoverability involves legacy directory decay and print archival truncation among multi-decade custom manufacturers. Cabinets By Nichols (row 1085, located at 40 W Old Plank Rd, Bargersville, IN 46106) has operated as a family-owned custom cabinet manufacturer since 1952, utilizing its primary Google Business Profile number (317) 670-0888 alongside a long-standing secondary plant line (317) 422-5188 and toll-free number (800) 422-3127. While Houzz records identify key principals Rob Nichols, David Blount, and Randy Bunton, and trade publications such as Kitchen & Bath Design News (September 2015 and May 2021 editions) reference an info@ email prefix, the indexed archival documents truncate or mask the domain portion of the address. In accordance with strict verification protocols that prohibit domain guessing or pattern reconstruction, no unverified email is recorded. Similarly, Dun & Bradstreet and Cylex business directory records for The Cabinet Brothers (row 1086, serving La Vergne and Nashville, TN from 1933 Elm Hill Pike) reference a truncated web string (https://www.cabinetstorenashville...) that no longer resolves as an active standalone site, while Dream Kitchens Plus (row 1090, Safford, AZ) and Spaulding Home Solutions LLC (row 1091, North Ridgeville, OH) exhibit inactive legacy domains (dreamkitchensplus.com, dream-kitchens-plus.business.site, and clevelandhomeremodeler.com) that have been superseded by Facebook business pages.Finally, the audited cohort demonstrates a pervasive shift toward social-first client acquisition and real-time mobile communication. Active remodelers such as River Valley Renovations (row 1092, Minden, LA), Spaulding Home Solutions LLC (row 1091, North Ridgeville, OH), AJ's Home Improvement Services (row 1094, Tonawanda, NY), Tremain's Top Tile (row 1097, Winona, MN), Cavinder Remodeling (row 1098, Hopkinsville, KY), and Blue Collar Countertops (row 1099, Mt Vernon, OH) regularly publish visual documentation of completed kitchen, bath, flooring, and exterior projects on Facebook. Digital Footprint and Contact Intelligence Report: U.S. Kitchen and Bathroom Remodeling Businesses (Rows 1184–1283)Executive Summary and Digital Attrition AnalysisAn exhaustive open-source intelligence (OSINT) investigation of small-to-medium kitchen, bath, cabinetry, and stone remodeling contractors across the United States (rows 1184 to 1283 of the target cohort) reveals pronounced structural fragmentation in how local trades maintain public digital infrastructure. Although every entity in the 100-business dataset maintains a Google Business Profile initially classified as lacking an active website, cross-referencing state corporate filings, federal transportation registries, supplier dealer locators, and home-service aggregators across the primary verification block (rows 1184–1228, $n = 45$) uncovered verified digital assets—live websites, defunct legacy domains, published contact emails, or official Facebook pages—for 21 businesses (46.7%), while 24 businesses (53.3%) operate exclusively through offline referrals and third-party directory citations.A primary structural driver of this fragmentation is high domain attrition coupled with platform decommissioning across the remodeling sector. Among the 15 businesses where a standalone web domain was identified, 9 domains (60.0%) are dead or decommissioned, compared to 6 live websites (40.0%). This attrition stems from two distinct mechanisms: Google's sunsetting of free .business.site subdomains—exemplified by Tile Works (tile-works-bathroom-remodeler.business.site)—and small contractors allowing custom domains (jakshomesupply.com, shurform.com, stoneandcabinets.com, midwesttopshop.com) to lapse after shifting lead generation to word-of-mouth referrals or third-party aggregator platforms. Conversely, several remodelers have launched modern web properties or cloud-hosted applications—such as Dream Kitchen & Bath (dream-kitchen-and-bath.vercel.app), Artline Kitchen & Bath (artlinekb.com), Creekside Cabinets (creeksidecabinetsut.com), Express Kitchen Cabinets (expresskitchenspro.com), Specialty Woodworks (specialtywoodworksandtile.com), and All About Kitchens (aakmodesto.com)—that have not yet been synchronized with their Google Business Profiles, indicating an ongoing lag between contractor web deployment and directory indexing.Beyond standalone web domains, small remodeling contractors rarely publish email addresses on general consumer directories, yet frequently expose verified operational emails on upstream B2B supplier networks and federal compliance databases. Specifically, DC Kitchen & Bath (dckitchenb@gmail.com) published its contact email on the Cosentino stone manufacturer dealer locator to capture manufacturer-referred countertop leads, while Uxbridge Cabinet Center (uxbcabctr@aol.com) published its email both on its Facebook profile and on Federal Motor Carrier Safety Administration (FMCSA) USDOT registration filings (USDOT 862167) required for operating commercial delivery trucks. Similarly, Compass Bath and Remodel embedded its direct Gmail address (russ.compassbathandremodel@gmail.com) and Facebook profile link directly inside its HomeAdvisor and Angi review listings, establishing a direct communication channel with prospective clients while bypassing aggregator lead-gating fees.Even where live social media profiles, email addresses, or active domains are absent, regulatory and trade registries provide high-confidence attribution of corporate leadership. Across the 24 businesses in rows 1184–1228 where no live social media URL, email, or website was recoverable, state contractor licensing boards (such as the Washington State Department of Labor & Industries and the Florida Division of Corporations), Better Business Bureau (BBB) records, and local press archives identified the governing principals for 8 additional firms, bringing total owner and principal identification to 16 of 45 researched businesses (35.6%). As digital lead-generation costs continue to rise on consumer portals, small remodeling enterprises are increasingly bifurcating into firms that invest in direct B2B dealer networks and cloud-hosted storefronts versus legacy trade shops that rely entirely on state license standing and local reputation.Verified Digital Assets and Contact Intelligence (Rows 1184–1228)Cross-referencing the 45 businesses in rows 1184 through 1228 against commercial, municipal, and trade directories demonstrates a clear operational divide between retail-facing showrooms and service-area-based specialty subcontractors. Retail cabinet and stone showrooms—such as Artline Kitchen & Bath in Ridgefield, New Jersey, Express Kitchen Cabinets in Hickory Hills, Illinois, Specialty Woodworks in Van Buren, Arkansas, and All About Kitchens in Modesto, California—maintain active web domains and public contact channels to support physical foot traffic and supplier partnerships. Conversely, long-tenured millwork shops and regional resurfacing specialists—such as Farrell's Top Shop & Cabinets in Athens, Tennessee (operating since 1984), Tim's Custom Cabinets in Sequim, Washington (registered since 1989), and Bathtub Wizard in Jermyn, Pennsylvania (operating since 2003)—rely almost entirely on established builder relationships, local radio sponsorships, and print classifieds rather than active web properties. Small Remodeling Contractor Digital Footprint and Contact Enrichment Report (Rows 1384–1483)Executive Summary and Digital Presence PatternsAn open-source intelligence (OSINT) and digital footprint audit of 100 small kitchen, bathroom, cabinetry, and tile remodeling businesses across the United States (rows 1384 to 1483, with exhaustive multi-directory deep verification completed through row 1418 and baseline structural profiling completed for rows 1419 to 1483) reveals a pronounced structural divide between physical showroom operators (72% of the 100-business cohort) and mobile service-area businesses (28% of the cohort). Although every business in the sample maintains a Google Business Profile (GBP) lacking an active website link on its primary map listing, cross-referencing state corporate registries, federal motor carrier safety records, public procurement contracts, supplier dealer locators, and social platforms uncovers verifiable digital endpoints—live websites, dead legacy domains, active Facebook business pages, or published contact emails—for 13 of the 35 deeply audited businesses (37.1%) in rows 1384–1418, alongside verified owner or officer identities for 11 of those 35 businesses (including 4 businesses that lack any web or social endpoints).A primary driver of apparent digital absence among small remodeling contractors is legacy domain attrition, contrasted against unlinked live showrooms. Among the eight standalone websites uncovered across rows 1384–1418, six—prestigiouskitchens.com (row 1386), prestigetileremodeling.com (row 1387), kyrahomes.com (row 1388), clintonfloorandcabinets.com (row 1390), moderncabinetdesigns.com (row 1396), and reddickmarbleandgranite.com (row 1399)—persist solely as dead URLs cached on third-party business directories such as Birdeye, Cybo, YellowPages, FabLocator, and Chamber of Commerce listings. This pattern indicates that small trade contractors frequently register custom domains during initial entity formation or marketing agency onboarding, only to let hosting and domain registrations lapse once referral networks stabilize and inbound lead generation consolidates around Google Business Profile telephone inquiries. Conversely, two brick-and-mortar design studios—House of Kitchens Ltd in Sioux City, Iowa (https://houseofkitchensltd.com/) and The Interior in Walla Walla, Washington (https://www.theinteriorwallawalla.com/)—maintain live, actively managed corporate websites that match their physical addresses and telephone numbers but remain omitted from their Google Business Profile metadata.Where standalone websites have either lapsed or never been commissioned, contractors frequently adopt a social-first portfolio model to showcase completed installations. Five businesses in the audited group—Prestige Tile Remodeling (row 1387), Garibay Flooring Co (row 1389), Shoals Kitchen and Bath, LLC (row 1400), Lowken Renovations LLC (row 1415), and Tommy Slade Construction and Remodeling (row 1417)—maintain verified Facebook business pages featuring job-site photography, phone verification, and regional service tags. Beyond dedicated business pages, micro-contractors also leverage regional Facebook community and trade groups as zero-cost advertising channels without maintaining a formal business page. For example, Norton Remodeling & Home Improvement (row 1406) distributes digital business card graphics for owner John Norton inside Maryville, Tennessee job boards and small-business groups, while J&A Custom Tile and Stone (row 1418) posts project descriptions and material specifications inside kitchen renovation interest groups.Furthermore, electronic mail discovery within the small remodeling sector rarely follows standard corporate contact-page conventions, instead surfacing through social media photo captions, public-sector procurement records, and GBP address-field workarounds. Garibay Flooring Co (row 1389) published its custom-domain email (crisg@garibayflooringco.com) directly inside a Facebook photo caption alongside its telephone number (281)640-4906, even though the underlying web domain is not operated as a public website. Independent cross-verification of Texas Health and Human Services Commission procurement records (Purchase Order #415190) for the same vendor in Brenham, Texas revealed owner Christopher Garibay's full legal name and secondary contact address (cristophergaribay17@icloud.com). In a distinct metadata workaround, CRUSH IT Renovations LLC in South Pasadena, Florida (row 1405) injected its contact email (crushitrenovate@gmail.com) directly into the street address string of its Google Business Profile (email. crushitrenovate@gmail.com, 7882 Sailboat Key Blvd S) to ensure visibility to map searchers in the absence of a website field. At the opposite end of the technological spectrum, traditional millwork enterprises such as Shady Grove Cabinet Shop in Greencastle, Pennsylvania (row 1385)—founded in 1999 and registered with the Federal Motor Carrier Safety Administration under USDOT #1009753—operate entirely offline, with industrial supply directories explicitly noting that the shop is not set up to receive electronic mail.Cross-directory address and telephone verification also exposes corporate white-labeling and franchise co-location among regional bath remodelers. In Bakersfield, California, Lowken Renovations LLC (row 1415) shares both its physical suite (3154 Landco Dr Suite D, Bakersfield, CA 93308) and its primary telephone line ((661) 328-0477) with Bath Planet of Bakersfield. Small Remodeling Contractor Digital Footprint and Contact Enrichment Report (Rows 1384–1483)Executive Summary and Digital Presence PatternsAn open-source intelligence (OSINT) and digital footprint audit of 100 small kitchen, bathroom, cabinetry, and tile remodeling businesses across the United States (rows 1384 to 1483, with exhaustive multi-directory deep verification completed through row 1418 and baseline structural profiling completed for rows 1419 to 1483) reveals a pronounced structural divide between physical showroom operators (72% of the 100-business cohort) and mobile service-area businesses (28% of the cohort). Although every business in the sample maintains a Google Business Profile (GBP) lacking an active website link on its primary map listing, cross-referencing state corporate registries, federal motor carrier safety records, public procurement contracts, supplier dealer locators, and social platforms uncovers verifiable digital endpoints—live websites, dead legacy domains, active Facebook business pages, or published contact emails—for 13 of the 35 deeply audited businesses (37.1%) in rows 1384–1418, alongside verified owner or officer identities for 11 of those 35 businesses (including 4 businesses that lack any web or social endpoints).A primary driver of apparent digital absence among small remodeling contractors is legacy domain attrition, contrasted against unlinked live showrooms. Among the eight standalone websites uncovered across rows 1384–1418, six—prestigiouskitchens.com (row 1386), prestigetileremodeling.com (row 1387), kyrahomes.com (row 1388), clintonfloorandcabinets.com (row 1390), moderncabinetdesigns.com (row 1396), and reddickmarbleandgranite.com (row 1399)—persist solely as dead URLs cached on third-party business directories such as Birdeye, Cybo, YellowPages, FabLocator, and Chamber of Commerce listings. This pattern indicates that small trade contractors frequently register custom domains during initial entity formation or marketing agency onboarding, only to let hosting and domain registrations lapse once referral networks stabilize and inbound lead generation consolidates around Google Business Profile telephone inquiries. Conversely, two brick-and-mortar design studios—House of Kitchens Ltd in Sioux City, Iowa (https://houseofkitchensltd.com/) and The Interior in Walla Walla, Washington (https://www.theinteriorwallawalla.com/)—maintain live, actively managed corporate websites that match their physical addresses and telephone numbers but remain omitted from their Google Business Profile metadata.Where standalone websites have either lapsed or never been commissioned, contractors frequently adopt a social-first portfolio model to showcase completed installations. Five businesses in the audited group—Prestige Tile Remodeling (row 1387), Garibay Flooring Co (row 1389), Shoals Kitchen and Bath, LLC (row 1400), Lowken Renovations LLC (row 1415), and Tommy Slade Construction and Remodeling (row 1417)—maintain verified Facebook business pages featuring job-site photography, phone verification, and regional service tags. Beyond dedicated business pages, micro-contractors also leverage regional Facebook community and trade groups as zero-cost advertising channels without maintaining a formal business page. For example, Norton Remodeling & Home Improvement (row 1406) distributes digital business card graphics for owner John Norton inside Maryville, Tennessee job boards and small-business groups, while J&A Custom Tile and Stone (row 1418) posts project descriptions and material specifications inside kitchen renovation interest groups.Furthermore, electronic mail discovery within the small remodeling sector rarely follows standard corporate contact-page conventions, instead surfacing through social media photo captions, public-sector procurement records, and GBP address-field workarounds. Garibay Flooring Co (row 1389) published its custom-domain email (crisg@garibayflooringco.com) directly inside a Facebook photo caption alongside its telephone number (281)640-4906, even though the underlying web domain is not operated as a public website. Independent cross-verification of Texas Health and Human Services Commission procurement records (Purchase Order #415190) for the same vendor in Brenham, Texas revealed owner Christopher Garibay's full legal name and secondary contact address (cristophergaribay17@icloud.com). In a distinct metadata workaround, CRUSH IT Renovations LLC in South Pasadena, Florida (row 1405) injected its contact email (crushitrenovate@gmail.com) directly into the street address string of its Google Business Profile (email. crushitrenovate@gmail.com, 7882 Sailboat Key Blvd S) to ensure visibility to map searchers in the absence of a website field. At the opposite end of the technological spectrum, traditional millwork enterprises such as Shady Grove Cabinet Shop in Greencastle, Pennsylvania (row 1385)—founded in 1999 and registered with the Federal Motor Carrier Safety Administration under USDOT #1009753—operate entirely offline, with industrial supply directories explicitly noting that the shop is not set up to receive electronic mail.Cross-directory address and telephone verification also exposes corporate white-labeling and franchise co-location among regional bath remodelers. In Bakersfield, California, Lowken Renovations LLC (row 1415) shares both its physical suite (3154 Landco Dr Suite D, Bakersfield, CA 93308) and its primary telephone line ((661) 328-0477) with Bath Planet of Bakersfield. Operating as the local legal entity and installation contractor behind a national acrylic bath-system franchise allows the business to maintain dual market-facing identities while consolidating administrative operations under founder Eric R. and office manager Vanessa Arreola.Detailed Audit of Researched Remodeling Businesses (Rows 1384–1418)The structured comparison below documents the complete verification record across all 35 businesses audited in rows 1384 through 1418, integrating regulatory filings, Better Business Bureau records, state contractor licenses, and principal identifications alongside the verified digital assets.RowBusiness NameTown & StatePhoneVerified Digital Endpoints & StatusOwner / Principal IdentifiedKey Verification Notes & Regulatory Context1384Oshkosh Kitchen RemodelerOshkosh, WI(920) 605-8180NoneUnknownListed on Maptons, Cybo, Wanderboat, and ContractorCREDS at 837 N Main St; Digital Footprint and Contact Verification Report: US Kitchen and Bathroom Remodeling Contractors (Part 08, Rows 1784–1824)Executive Summary and Digital Presence PatternsAn exhaustive open-source audit of 41 small kitchen, bathroom, cabinetry, and surface remodeling contractors across the United States (rows 1784 through 1824 of Part 08) demonstrates a pronounced structural divide between local operational longevity and active first-party digital infrastructure. Although every business in the cohort maintains a Google Business Profile without a functional website link on its primary map listing, deeper cross-referencing across state corporate registries, contractor licensing boards, social platforms, and trade directories uncovered verifiable digital assets—including live unlinked websites, legacy defunct domains, active Facebook business pages, or published direct email addresses—for 15 of the 41 enterprises (36.6%), while 26 businesses (63.4%) operate exclusively via telephone and third-party lead aggregators.Structural Mechanisms of Digital Attrition and DiscoveryA primary driver explaining why established local remodeling contractors appear offline on their Google Business Profiles is widespread domain attrition compounded by platform-level sunsetting. Seven of the 15 businesses with discovered web records—representing 46.7% of all positive matches—previously registered custom domains or Google Business Site URLs that have since lapsed into defunct status. For instance, Top Shop in Mountain Home, Arkansas (row 1792), relied on top-shop-cabinet-maker.business.site, a domain rendered inoperable following Google's discontinuation of .business.site web hosting. Similarly, long-standing fabricators and remodelers such as Barry's Cultured Marble in Benicia, California (row 1820, licensed since 2002 under CSLB #809633), Signature Cabinets LLC in Florence, Alabama (row 1789, operating for 24 years), and Interiors That Work in Longwood, Florida (row 1806) allowed their custom domains to expire while maintaining active brick-and-mortar showrooms and state corporate registrations. As domain renewal costs and web maintenance overhead rise relative to word-of-mouth referrals, veteran tradespeople increasingly abandon standalone websites in favor of passive directory presence, a trajectory that leaves legacy URLs stranded across secondary aggregators such as Birdeye, YellowPages, and Cybo.Alongside expired domains, metadata fragmentation frequently obscures live web infrastructure when multi-location operators or micro-site users fail to synchronize their URLs with their local map profiles. Three audited contractors maintain live, functional websites that remain completely unlinked from their Google Business Profiles. HSP Kitchen Remodel in Lakewood, Washington (row 1786), operates as a regional branch of Home Services Plus (homeservicesplus.pro), which maintains dedicated showroom and service-area landing pages across Pierce and Snohomish counties. Similarly, Guntersville Granite in Albertville, Alabama (row 1791), runs a live corporate website (guntersvillegranite.com) confirming its Glover Street manufacturing facility and telephone lines, while Mario's Home Pro Services in Green Cove Springs, Florida (row 1824), utilizes a hosted micro-site (marioshomeproservicesinc.zumvu.com) alongside its active Florida corporate registration. In these instances, decentralized digital marketing management results in orphaned map listings that underrepresent the contractor's true online footprint.Furthermore, because small remodeling contractors rarely publish plain-text email addresses on their own social profiles or websites to avoid automated spam, their direct electronic contact information most reliably surfaces through local business-to-business referral ecosystems and social portfolio páginas. Four businesses maintain verified Facebook pages to showcase project photography (shafferconstructiondelmarva, AlexanderMassaLLC, braunhomeimprovements, and tabinetscabinets), yet direct email discovery depended on partner networks. Specifically, kelly@kellyskandb.com for Kelly's Kitchen and Bath in O'Fallon, Missouri (row 1816), was published in a collaborative project showcase post by Kelley Home Services alongside the exact telephone number (636-697-0380), while tabinets@gmail.com for Tabinets in Lakeville, Massachusetts (row 1823), was published on a local real estate brokerage's preferred vendor directory alongside owner Tab Shumway's name and telephone number 774-419-2171. Consequently, cross-referencing allied local trades and real estate partner pages remains essential for verifying contractor contact pathways when first-party web properties are absent.Comprehensive Audit Table (Rows 1784–1824)The table below summarizes the verification results, corporate ownership records, licensing data, and directory cross-checks across all 41 businesses evaluated in this batch.RowBusiness NameTownVerified Asset StatusKey Findings, Ownership & Directory Cross-Check1784Imperial Home Remodeling - Tile&Bathroom RemodelCitrus Heights, CANothing FoundListed on Houzz across Sacramento/Roseville/Folsom; a Facebook group post references a different NJ phone (856-302-6207), so no page was accepted.1785Shaffer ConstructionSalisbury, MDFacebook FoundVerified page facebook.com/shafferconstructiondelmarva includes phone 443-859-5317 in title; 243 likes; latest post Nov 28, 2022.1786HSP Kitchen RemodelLakewood, WALive WebsiteLive site homeservicesplus.pro/kitchen-remodel-showroom-tacoma/ covers the Lakewood/Tacoma office and showroom.1787Total Upgrade & RemodelingBloomingdale, ILNothing FoundVerified address (One Tiffany Pointe Ste 300) and phone (630-205-6254) on WhosMyPro; no social, email, or web presence.1788Porcelain Refinishing CompanyRedlands, CANothing FoundBBB, Angi, and HomeAdvisor confirm phone (909-735-5151) and owners Jared P. Digital Footprint and Contact Intelligence Report: Small U.S. Kitchen and Bathroom Remodeling Contractors (Rows 1884–1983)Executive Analysis of Contractor Digital PresenceAn exhaustive multi-source investigation of small kitchen, cabinetry, tile, and bathroom remodeling businesses across the United States—covering rows 1884 through 1951 in depth prior to search capacity truncation at row 1952—reveals a pronounced structural divide in how local tradesmen maintain their digital infrastructure. Although every business in the cohort operates an active Google Business Profile without a linked functional website on its primary map listing, 23 of the 68 investigated contractors (33.8%) possess verifiable off-profile digital assets, comprising 7 self-published email addresses, 7 confirmed Facebook business pages, 6 live standalone websites not linked to their Google profiles, and 10 defunct or expired custom domains. Furthermore, among the 45 researched businesses lacking a qualifying social media page, email address, or custom domain, state corporate registries, Better Business Bureau (BBB) files, trade publications, and local directory platforms yielded verified principal ownership, state contractor licensing, or physical address intelligence for an additional 16 entities, bringing total ownership and operational identification to 26 of the 68 researched businesses.A primary dynamic shaping the digital footprint of this cohort is the high rate of custom domain attrition contrasted against a subset of live, functional websites that remain unlinked to Google Maps. Among the 16 custom domains discovered for businesses flagged as lacking a website on their Google Business Profiles, 10 (62.5%) are expired or offline, including macombkitchenandbath.com (row 1885), bigbearcabinetry.co (row 1886), affordablecabinetsllc.com (row 1895), fortheloveofcabinets.com (row 1897), and joslinscabinetco.com (row 1945). In the residential remodeling trade, small proprietors frequently register a domain during initial business formation or a local chamber of commerce ribbon-cutting ceremony, allow the web hosting subscription to lapse once word-of-mouth referrals stabilize, and shift their customer communication entirely to mobile telephone lines and sporadic Facebook photo updates. Conversely, six businesses—Kraft Kitchens (kraftkitchensdesign.com, row 1907), Iron Peak Remodeling (ironpeakremodeling.com, row 1919), Countertop Concepts (mtcountertopconcepts.com, row 1923), Top Notch Renovations (top-notchrenovations.com, row 1925), Bath Rescue, Inc. (bathrescue.net, row 1934), and Anointed Action Services (anointedactionservices.godaddysites.com, row 1935)—maintain live websites that were never synchronized with their Google Business Profiles, reflecting a persistent listing-management disconnect that limits organic search conversion.Cross-referencing telephone numbers and street addresses across municipal, licensing, and commercial databases also exposes substantial operational fluidity, service-line pivoting, and multi-entity infrastructure sharing. In Madison, Wisconsin, Top Notch Renovations (row 1925) maintains a bathroom remodeling Google profile and a general contractor BBB file under owner Jay Miller, yet its live website (top-notchrenovations.com) and recent directory descriptions have pivoted to commercial and residential HVAC maintenance. Even more pronounced infrastructure sharing occurs in Springfield, Missouri, where the telephone number (417) 353-0990 and physical address 851 S. Glenstone Ave listed for Springfield Kitchen and Bathroom Remodeling Company (row 1894) are simultaneously utilized in city business licenses and classified advertisements for a martial arts school (Sameday Jiu Jitsu, associated with Ron Bass and Michael Rose), a recording studio (Soar High Records), and a regional appliance and automotive repair operation (Samedayservice.com). Similarly, Anointed Action Services (row 1935) in Richton Park, Illinois operates both a residential carpentry and home improvement service linked to Birloather Mathena and an interstate motor carrier entity (MC-1658743-C) registered with federal transportation authorities.Finally, the investigation demonstrates that directory metadata leakage serves as the primary vector for discovering self-published contractor email addresses when standalone websites are absent. Small remodeling contractors rarely display direct email addresses on major lead-generation portals such as Angi, HomeAdvisor, or Houzz, because those platforms intentionally mask contact details to monetize lead routing. Instead, self-published emails surface on municipal chamber of commerce pages, secondary business aggregators, and social media post captions. Notably, the Polson Chamber of Commerce directory published ccmtops@gmail.com for Countertop Concepts (row 1923) and inadvertently exposed the company's unlinked Facebook profile ID (61578875853805) within a raw WordPress database array dump on its category listing page. Similarly, regional directories preserved en071591@gmail.com on USdirectory for M&J Demolition & Remodeling LLC (row 1905) and texaspridehomeservices@gmail.com on LocalHorizon for Texas Pride Home Services (row 1941), while an international construction directory scraper captured topnotch.renovatios@gmail.com (retaining that exact spelling) for Top Notch Renovations (row 1925) and a Facebook photo caption provided IndependenceRenovationsLLC@gmail.com for Independence Renovations LLC (row 1909).Supplemental Intelligence on Non-CSV Remodeling BusinessesFor 16 of the 45 researched businesses that did not yield a qualifying Facebook page, Instagram profile, self-published email, or website for the final CSV block, state corporate filings, contractor licensing boards, trade magazines, and customer review platforms established verified ownership, licensing, or entity-disambiguation data as detailed in the table below.RowBusiness NameTown & StateOwner / Principal IdentifiedSupplemental Licensing, Address & Disambiguation Findings1887David's Custom CabinetsFranklin, VADavid Lowe (Owner); P. Digital Footprint Verification and Lead Enrichment Report: U.S. Kitchen and Bathroom Remodeling Cohort (Rows 1984–2083)Executive Summary and Cohort ArchitectureSmall and mid-sized residential remodeling contractors operating without an active website linked to their Google Business Profile (GBP) exhibit a highly fragmented digital footprint that rarely aligns with conventional corporate directories. Across the 100-business cohort spanning rows 1984 through 2083, 76 contractors operate from designated physical addresses or showrooms while 24 operate as residential service-area businesses (SABs), distributed across 31 states with primary concentrations in California (18 businesses), Illinois (8 businesses), Florida (7 businesses), North Carolina (5 businesses), and Georgia, Indiana, Missouri, Pennsylvania, Texas, and Wisconsin (4 businesses each). This structural split reflects the historical evolution of the domestic remodeling trade, wherein established millwork shops and design showrooms anchor commercial corridors while independent finish carpenters and tile installers operate mobile fleets out of residentially zoned properties.A rigorous multi-channel verification audit conducted across the initial 31 contiguous businesses (rows 1984 through 2014) and row 2018 demonstrates that while direct consumer-facing web assets are sparse, deep trade and municipal registries consistently expose verified ownership, licensing, and high-intent contact pathways. Specifically, among the 31 fully audited businesses (rows 1984–2014), 10 entities (32.3%) yielded verified digital contact assets—including two live standalone websites that were unlinked from their Google Business Profiles, four historical or parked web domains preserved in business directories and classified advertisements, three verified Facebook business pages, and three published email addresses. Even among contractors lacking public social media or web domains, cross-referencing municipal building permit logs, state contractor licensing boards, and corporate registries identified the legal principals for 14 of the 31 audited businesses (45.2%) through permit records, trade and corporate filings, and verified commercial directories.Cross-Channel Mechanisms of Contractor DiscoverabilityThe empirical findings from the audited subset reveal three structural mechanisms governing how small remodeling contractors maintain commercial visibility without an active Google Business Profile website link, offering clear implications for future B2B lead enrichment and contractor outreach.First, B2B manufacturer and wholesale supplier dealer locators serve as the highest-integrity repository for verified contractor emails and active web domains that have become decoupled from Google Maps. Established showroom operators frequently maintain updated dealer profiles with cabinet and hardware manufacturers to receive qualified referrals, even when their unclaimed or legacy GBP listings omit website URLs. For example, House Splendid (Row 2006, Emmaus, PA) had no website linked on its GBP, yet hardware manufacturer Colonial Bronze published its direct owner email (lia@housesplendid.com), which in turn corresponded to a fully operational design studio website (https://www.housesplendid.com/) run by principal Lia Papasimakis Fraccaro. Similarly, R L Design (Row 2007, Sterling, CO) published its active Yahoo business email (rldesign1@yahoo.com) on the Custom Wood Products (cwponline.com) premier dealer portal while operating a live website under a hyphenated domain (https://www.design-rl.com/) managed by principal Roger Lambrecht. Even when a remodeler has no public email, supplier project showcases confirm commercial activity, as seen with Nu Look Kitchens (Row 2011, Lindenhurst, IL) featuring custom black solid-tone cabinetry in the WalzCraft customer showcase. Going forward, programmatic scraping of upstream cabinet, countertop, and architectural hardware dealer networks represents a far higher-yield discovery mechanism for brick-and-mortar showrooms than generic web search.Second, social media substitution and community group prospecting frequently replace standalone websites for local craft remodelers. Rather than paying for ongoing domain hosting and web maintenance—a common cause of expired or dead domains such as nordicdesigncustomtile.com (Row 1986), deviandesigns.com (Row 1988), kitchenandbathdirect.net (Row 1989), and remodelerelpaso-tx.com (Row 1990)—small contractors rely on Facebook photo albums and local trade groups to demonstrate workmanship. Mohawk Renovations, LLC (Row 1987, Aurora, IL) utilizes Facebook video reels (https://www.facebook.com/Mohawkrenovation) to showcase completed interior renovations, while Precision Woodworks LLC (Row 2012, Suffolk, VA) operates under a personal-handle business page (https://www.facebook.com/stephenjames48) explicitly titled with its full trade name, town, and phone number (Precision Woodworks LLC Custom Kitchens and Baths Suffolk Va 757-642-1686). In other cases, contractors forgo a dedicated page URL entirely and rely on regional "need a contractor" Facebook groups and marketplace platforms to bid on jobs directly: Chad Loupe of Loupe Woodcraft (Row 2003, Houma, LA) markets via local Houma community boards and Thumbtack, while Marc Baker of On Point Home Remodeling (Row 2013, Decatur, IL) actively solicits deck and bathroom remodeling estimates inside central Illinois Facebook groups using his business phone (217-369-7986).Third, municipal permit logs, state licensing boards, and Chamber of Commerce directories expose the legal entity and principal behind trade names, while simultaneously filtering out "lead-gen ghost listings". Established brick-and-mortar and trade contractors leave dense regulatory trails: D&S Interiors (Row 1985, Roanoke/Hoagland, IN) has 50 recorded building permits on BuildZoom under D and S Interiors Plus LLC with scheduling contact Kara (260-410-3937); Made New Construction LLC (Row 1997, Rathdrum, ID) holds active Washington L&I license MADENNC746DQ (UBI 606 123 285) and Idaho registration 5371393 under managing partner Cameron Jason Williams; and Calhoun Millworks (Row 2001, Calhoun, GA) operates an interstate commercial fleet under USDOT 2952998 and parent entity Schellhorn Enterprises LLC, led by president Alfred E. Schellhorn. Local civic associations also bridge the digital divide, as illustrated by the Jackson Chamber of Commerce publishing the contact email (swissarmy@element74.com) for presidents Steve and Greg Beussink of Beussink Brothers Woodworks (Row 1995, Jackson, MO). Conversely, generic geo-keyword listings such as San Clemente Bathroom Remodeling Pros (Row 1991, San Clemente, CA) and Concord Kitchen Remodeling (Row 2005, Concord, CA) exhibit zero regulatory, licensing, or municipal permit records, appearing exclusively on automated reverse-phone-lookup scraper networks or conflicting with unrelated regional domains. Distinguishing verified trade operators from lead-generation front listings is essential for maintaining outreach deliverability and database hygiene.Comprehensive Verification Audit of Researched Businesses (Rows 1984–2014 & 2018)The following table synthesizes the complete evidentiary record across all 32 researched businesses (Rows 1984–2014 and Row 2018), detailing verified digital endpoints, legal entity structures, identified principals, and regulatory or directory footprints.RowBusiness Name & LocationPhoneVerified Digital Footprint (Web / Social / Email)Legal Entity, Principal(s) & Regulatory / Directory Findings1984Trusty Hammers of NWILake Station, IN (SAB)(219) 240-5208None foundPhone number appears in City of Hobart municipal contractor/flooring document lists (cityofhobart.org); no social or web presence.1985D&S InteriorsRoanoke, IN(260) 410-3937None foundOperates as D and S Interiors Plus LLC (also listed at 14407 Marion Center Rd, Hoagland, IN & 5923 Brightleaf Ct). 50 building permits on BuildZoom; permit inspection logs list contact Kara (260-410-3937) and Brian W..1986Nordic Design Custom HomesTroy, MO(636) 462-0803Dead Website: http://nordicdesigncustomtile.comAlso trades as Nordic Design and Tile. Digital Footprint Verification and Contact Endpoint Analysis of Small Kitchen and Bathroom Remodeling Contractors (Rows 2084–2183)Executive Summary and Cohort OverviewSmall-scale kitchen, bathroom, and specialty residential remodeling businesses across the United States exhibit a pronounced structural disconnect between local search visibility on Google Business Profile (GBP) and owned digital infrastructure. Across the 100 remodeling businesses cataloged in Part 11 (rows 2084 to 2183), 67.0% (67 entities) register physical street or postal addresses while 33.0% (33 entities) operate as unaddressed service-area businesses (SABs). Within the deeply audited segment of 34 businesses (rows 2084 through 2117), primary source verification across state corporate registries, contractor licensing boards, Better Business Bureau (BBB) records, trade directories, and social platforms yielded verified contact or web endpoints for 9 businesses (26.5%) and confirmed principal ownership, state licensing, or third-party directory records for an additional 20 businesses (58.8%), leaving only 5 businesses (14.7%) with no external digital trace beyond their base map listing.Among the 9 businesses with verified web, email, or social media endpoints in rows 2084–2117, 2 businesses maintain active, live websites with published contact email addresses (Anderson Home Renovations at https://www.ahr-renovation.com/ with anderson904923@gmail.com, and Intrinsic Elite Services LLC at https://www.iesfl.com/ with info@iesfl.com), 2 businesses maintain verified Facebook pages (Anderson Home Renovations and Paul Allen Construction), and 6 businesses retain historical or defunct website URLs (W M Enterprise Custom Woodworking, Home Renovation Solution, Whatever It Takes Remodeling, Legacy Safe Homes, South Texas Supreme Remodel, and JD Total Renovation). None of the audited businesses maintained an active, verifiable Instagram profile linked to its business name, phone number, or principal owner. This divergence between high third-party directory syndication and low owned domain maintenance indicates that micro-contractors in the residential remodeling sector rely primarily on aggregator ecosystems and offline referral networks rather than self-managed web assets to sustain deal flow.Structural Dynamics of Small Remodeler Digital PresencePlatform Deprecation and Domain AttritionA close examination of the 6 dead website domains uncovered across rows 2084–2117 reveals three distinct mechanisms of digital attrition among micro-contractors. First, two Texas contractors—Whatever It Takes Remodeling (row 2098, http://whatever-it-takes-remodeling.business.site) and South Texas Supreme Remodel (row 2112, http://south-texas-supreme-remodel.business.site)—relied on Google's free .business.site web builder, which Google permanently sunsetted, automatically stripping those businesses of a working web property while leaving stale URL pointers across third-party aggregators such as Cybo. Second, Home Renovation Solution (row 2094, http://homerenovationsolution.localsearch.com) utilized a directory-hosted subdomain on LocalSearch via YellowPages that no longer resolves as an active standalone site. Third, established firms that previously registered custom top-level .com domains—W M Enterprise Custom Woodworking (row 2087, http://www.wmenterpriseltd.com), Legacy Safe Homes (row 2099, http://legacysafehomes.com), and JD Total Renovation (row 2117, http://jdtotalrenovation.com)—allowed their custom domains to lapse or go offline while continuing to operate via telephone and third-party lead aggregators. Because small remodeling firms frequently experience multi-month project backlogs from repeat residential clients, domain renewal and web hosting maintenance are often deprioritized once a steady local referral pipeline is established.Legacy Reputation Versus Lead-Generation Naming PatternsComparing operational tenure against business naming conventions exposes a sharp bifurcation in the dataset between multi-decade, owner-operated trade contractors and keyword-optimized local map listings. Established family and sole-proprietor firms routinely substitute offline reputation and trade directory credentials for social media or web marketing. For instance, B&B Unique Remodeling (row 2116, West Roxbury, MA), headed by third-generation contractor Scott Bourgeois, has operated since January 1991 (35 years) with 6 employees, a BuildZoom score of 167 (16 verified reviews, 5.0 rating), and an active Massachusetts license, yet maintains no standalone website or social media feed. Similarly, Cherry Homes, Inc. (row 2103, Harper Woods, MI), incorporated in March 1998 by owner Jeff Cherry, holds BBB Accreditation with an A+ rating since 2006, 132 reviews on HomeAdvisor (4.7 stars), and 156 reviews on Angi (4.3 stars), operating entirely through direct referrals and lead marketplaces. In Alabama, Sharp Remodeling Specialty. (row 2089, Ranburne, AL), owned by Glenn Sharp under Alabama Home Builders Licensure Board #17225, has served the Birmingham–Trussville–Ranburne corridor for over 25 years, relying on local print advertising in The Homewood Star and Houzz portfolios rather than a dedicated domain. Likewise, W M Enterprise Custom Woodworking (row 2087, Zanesville, OH), operated by Wayne since 2001, and Martin Residential Upgrades (row 2091, Lebanon, PA), operated by Richard under Pennsylvania license HIC # PA143975 (BuildZoom score 92), sustain high customer satisfaction ratings purely through word-of-mouth repeat clientele.On the opposite side of this structural divide are keyword-stuffed, geographically segmented Google Business Profiles designed primarily for local map pack capture. Across the full 100-business dataset, 6 separate listings in the Boise–Meridian–Eagle, Idaho metropolitan area share identical naming syntax: H&A Remodeler | Home, Deck & Kitchen Remodeling Highlands (row 2086), Harrison Boulevard Remodeler | Home Remodeling (row 2100), East End Remodeler | Home Remodeling (row 2101), H&A Remodeler | Home & Kitchen Remodeling Mesa H&A (row 2123), H&A Remodeler | Home Remodeling Meridian (row 2164), and H&A Remodeler | Home, Deck & Kitchen Remodeling Eagle (row 2175). Similar city-keyword constructions appear in California, Illinois, and Florida—such as Hero Bathroom Remodeling Torrance (row 2090), Blissful Bathroom Remodel San Francisco (row 2127), Elite Kitchen Remodeling Northwest Suburbs (row 2130), Pompano Beach Remodeling Kitchen and Bathroom Expert (row 2136), and US Kitchen Bath Bathroom Remodeling Thousand Oaks (row 2150)—which universally lack state corporate filings or historical trade directory footprints under those exact strings. As search engines tighten verification standards for service-area and multi-location entities, these uncorroborated keyword profiles face elevated suspension risk compared to state-licensed legacy operators.Anomalies in Recovered Live Websites and Area CodesEven where live websites and published emails are recovered, close inspection reveals operational nuances regarding physical location and site maintenance. Anderson Home Renovations (row 2096) lists Fayetteville, TN on its service-area GBP, whereas its live website (https://www.ahr-renovation.com/) and Facebook page (https://www.facebook.com/100088869777736) establish its physical base in neighboring Elora, TN (127A Wells Lee Rd, Elora, TN 37328) and publish the owner's direct email (anderson904923@gmail.com). Conversely, Intrinsic Elite Services LLC (row 2114, Crestview, FL), registered on Florida Sunbiz (L22000084065) to Phillip N. Bond at 123 Strike Eagle Dr, Crestview, FL 32536, operates a live HostPapa-built website (https://www.iesfl.com/) displaying its exact phone number (850-842-8255) and published email (info@iesfl.com), but the site body contains unedited template text and digital marketing agency copy ("Build a standout brand that fuels business growth") rather than remodeling portfolios. Furthermore, several contractors operate with out-of-market telephone area codes—such as LGR Remodeling LLC in Burlington, NC using a Memphis, TN (901) number (row 2115), Happy Home Remodeling, LLC in Post Falls, ID using a Denver, CO (720) number (row 2122), Elite Kitchen Remodeling Northwest Suburbs in Schaumburg, IL using an Arkansas (870) number (row 2130), Moyer Modern Construction in Bozeman, MT using an eastern Pennsylvania (484) number (row 2133), and Pompano Beach Remodeling Kitchen and Bathroom Expert in Pompano Beach, FL using a southwest Florida (239) number (row 2136)—reflecting either owner relocation with mobile number retention or virtual call-routing numbers.Comprehensive Audit of Researched Businesses (Rows 2084–2117)Summary of Verified Contact and Web Endpoints (9 Businesses)The table below details the 9 businesses within rows 2084–2117 for which at least one verified website (live or dead), Facebook business page, or published email address was recovered during multi-source investigation.RowBusiness NameTown & StatePhoneFacebook URLPublished EmailWebsite FoundWebsite StatusPrincipal / OwnerKey Verification Details2087W M Enterprise Custom WoodworkingZanesville, OH(740) 452-2325UNKNOWNUNKNOWNhttp://www.wmenterpriseltd.comdeadWayneOperating since 2001 (5.0 stars, 7 reviews on Google/Birdeye); Digital Footprint and Contact Verification Analysis: U.S. Kitchen and Bathroom Remodeling Contractors (Rows 2184–2283)Executive Analysis and Digital Presence PatternsSmall-scale kitchen, bathroom, and custom cabinetry contractors across the United States exhibit a pronounced structural divide between local statutory registration and active digital discoverability. Across the 100 remodeling enterprises cataloged in Part 12 (rows 2184 through 2283), 83 operate from fixed commercial, industrial, or residential-workshop addresses while 17 operate as mobile service-area businesses. Detailed multi-source verification across the first 43 enterprises (rows 2184 through 2226)—spanning municipal license registries, state corporate filings, Better Business Bureau (BBB) records, trade directories, and social platforms—reveals that while many contractors maintain active state registrations or third-party directory profiles, their independent web domains suffer from high attrition rates.Among the 43 audited businesses, exactly 12 (27.9%) possess at least one verifiable website, social media profile, or business-published email address. A critical phenomenon observed in this segment is domain abandonment: while only two businesses—Timeless Interiors in Grayslake, Illinois (https://timelessinteriorsshowroom.com/contact-us) and Cabinetry Solutions of the Palm Beaches LLC in Lantana, Florida (https://palmbeachcabinetry.com/)—maintain live, functioning websites that were omitted from their Google Business Profiles, eight businesses have historical domains still cited across third-party aggregators (such as Birdeye, YellowPages, Cybo, and KBF Market) that are now expired, unindexed, or defunct. In particular, contractors that previously relied on Google's free business.site subdomain infrastructure—such as Hast Building and Remodeling LLC (https://hast-building-and-remodeling-llc.business.site)—lost their web presence entirely following the platform's discontinuation of that service, leaving orphaned links across international directory mirrors. Over the medium term, this domain attrition forces local tradespeople into deeper reliance on aggregator platforms and social networks while severing direct organic search discovery.Furthermore, email discoverability in the small remodeling sector is heavily constrained by spam-avoidance behavior. Even when businesses maintain live websites or active Facebook pages, they overwhelmingly rely on web contact forms, telephone calls, or direct social messaging rather than exposing raw email strings in page text. The sole verified, business-affiliated email address in the audited group—karen4timelessinteriors@gmail.com for Timeless Interiors—was uncovered not on the company's live showroom website, which uses a reCAPTCHA-protected form, but inside the Village of Grayslake's 2025 municipal business registration ledger. Two other candidate email addresses illustrate the importance of strict entity disambiguation: in Sun City, Arizona, 3 Day Kitchens of Arizona (11210 W Pueblo Ct, (623) 512-5893) shares its exact physical address and telephone line with a co-located pool equipment company, Atlantis Saltwater Pool Chlorinators (https://atlantissaltwaterchlorinators.com/), which publishes saltwaterchlorinators@gmail.com; in Old Lyme, Connecticut, an email associated with William H. Robinson (william.robinson@comcast.net) appears exclusively on a consumer people-search aggregator (unmask.com) rather than a business-published source, requiring exclusion under verification standards.Comprehensive Verification Audit (Rows 2184–2226)Businesses with Verified Digital Assets (CSV Cohort)Twelve businesses in the audited cohort yielded at least one confirmed Facebook profile, live website, dead website domain, or published email address. Kitchen and Bathroom Remodeling Contractor Research Report (Rows 2284–2383)
All 100 kitchen, bathroom, and cabinetry remodeling businesses in Part 13 (rows 2284 to 2383) were investigated across social platforms, state licensing databases, business registries, local chambers of commerce, parish bulletins, and contractor directories. Across the 100 businesses researched, 44 businesses yielded at least one verified digital asset—a Facebook page, a published business email address, or a live or dead website—while 56 businesses had no verifiable social media profile, published business email, or standalone website beyond third-party directory listings.   

Specifically, the research uncovered 17 verified Facebook business pages, 17 published contact email addresses (strictly excluding people-search and email-finder aggregators), 11 live websites, and 20 dead or discontinued websites (such as expired custom domains or retired .business.site pages). Third-party directory subdomains (such as .localsearch.com pages on YellowPages for rows 2322, 2329, and 2354) were excluded per the website verification rules. Where directory listings or state records identified owner or contact names for businesses that otherwise lacked a standalone website, published email, or social page—such as Mark Appel for Old Mill Cabinets & Carpentry (row 2286), Sean J. Small Kitchen and Bathroom Remodeling Businesses Research Report: Part 14 (Rows 2384 to 294)All 100 kitchen and bathroom remodeling businesses in Part 14 (rows 2384 to 294) have been researched in exact sequential order across social platforms, business directories, state corporate and licensing registries, and web domains. Across the 100 businesses evaluated, 44 businesses yielded at least one verified digital asset (a published contact email, Facebook page, Instagram profile, or live/dead website domain), while 56 businesses yielded no qualifying social media, email, or website assets and are cataloged in the NOTHING_FOUND list at the end of this report.Among the 44 businesses included in the CSV output below, verified Facebook pages or business profiles were identified for 25 businesses, and official Instagram accounts were confirmed for 3 businesses (Dr. Cabinet NYC, Sloan Stone/ south coast stone via its showroom brand Coastal Kitchen & Bath, and Kapal Cabinets, Granite & Quartz via Kapal Industries). Directly published business or owner contact emails were verified for 14 businesses, each documented with its source URL and a verbatim quote of 20 words or fewer, while personal emails appearing exclusively on people-search or skip-tracing aggregators (such as FamilyTreeNow, TruePeopleSearch, or FastPeopleSearch) were excluded per task rules. US Kitchen and Bathroom Remodeling Businesses: Contact and Digital Footprint Research (Part 15, Rows 365 to 29)Across the 100 small kitchen and bathroom remodeling contractors evaluated in Part 15 (rows 365 to 29), 49 businesses yielded at least one verified digital asset—a published business email, an official Facebook page, an Instagram profile, or a live/defunct standalone website—while 51 businesses had no verifiable standalone web, email, or social media footprint beyond third-party directory aggregators. Specifically, the research uncovered 15 published business email addresses, 20 verified Facebook pages, 2 Instagram profiles, 14 live websites, and 17 dead or expired websites (including defunct .business.site domains and unindexed custom domains).Because many small remodeling contractors operate as service-area businesses without maintaining active custom domains, their published contact emails frequently reside on alternative digital surfaces rather than traditional company homepages. Among the 15 verified email addresses, 8 were published directly on live company websites (including Wix, Houzz Pro, and custom domains), while the remaining 7 were recovered from Facebook intro sections, USDOT/FMCSA commercial vehicle registrations, local home-builder association sponsor rosters, regional trade directories, and verified review-platform profiles. Round 2 Task 2 Part 17: Remodeling Business Contact and Digital Footprint Research (Rows 519 to 553)
Across the 96 small kitchen and bathroom remodeling businesses researched in Part 17 (covering rows 519 to 553 in the prescribed table order), 40 businesses yielded at least one verified digital asset—a published email address, an active or defunct company website, a Facebook business page, or an Instagram profile—while 56 businesses maintained only directory, licensing, or Google Business Profile footprints with no independent social media, email, or web domain. Specifically, the CSV below documents 11 verified business email addresses with direct source quotes, 16 Facebook business pages (14 standalone pages and 2 website-linked pages), 1 Instagram profile, 9 live company websites (including 1 rebranded company website operating at the same address and phone number, plus a secondary live Weebly blog noted on row 534), and 20 dead website entries (comprising 18 defunct or retired .business.site domains and 2 parked or "coming soon" pages) across 44 businesses with identified owners or principals.   

Strict verification standards were applied across all 96 records to prevent false-positive attributions. Candidate emails, social pages, or domains that shared a company name in a different city (such as Precision Craft Woodworks in Austin, Texas vs. Terrell, Texas, or Enliven Remodeling in Durham, North Carolina vs. Newman Lake, Washington) or shared a phone number or owner under a different legal entity name (such as Lusso Stone Design LLC in Miami, Florida sharing (305) 975-3777 with Custom Kitchen & Bath Stone in Cutler Bay, Florida, and J & Sons Contracting LLC sharing (503) 931-7461 and owner Jose Mosqueda with Smith Rock Interiors LLC in Salem/Keizer, Oregon) were excluded from the primary CSV fields and documented in the investigative table below.

Digital Infrastructure and Omnichannel Footprint Analysis of Independent Residential Remodeling Contractors
Empirical Analysis of Contractor Online Presence and Attrition
The residential specialty trade contracting sector—specifically micro-enterprises engaged in kitchen and bathroom remodeling—exhibits structural variance in digital adoption, domain maintenance, and public communication architecture. Rather than maintaining centralized, authoritative web properties, independent remodeling contractors display divergent operational trajectories, segregating into active omnichannel adopters, legacy infrastructure maintainers, and platform-dependent or offline traditionalists. The operational longevity of small remodeling enterprises does not correlate directly with digital sophistication; established firms routinely sustain commercial viability through commercial supplier referrals, localized word-of-mouth networks, and physical trade presence without active self-hosted websites.   

The digital assets of small remodeling contractors frequently follow a distinct decay pattern. Enterprises often register custom domains during incorporation or initial marketing pushes, only to abandon them when hosting expenses, technical maintenance hurdles, or localized demand cycles make independent maintenance economically unviable. Secondary data aggregators, trade directories, and municipal licensing databases preserve these stale digital references long after domain registration lapses, creating persistent digital ghost assets that diverge from the actual operational status of the enterprise.

Infrastructure Modalities and Operational Trajectories
The transition of remodeling businesses away from proprietary digital domains toward third-party ecosystems is evident across diverse commercial jurisdictions. Commercial domain lifecycles fall into distinct classifications characterized by active commercial deployments, legacy subdomain hosting, secondary domain market recycling, and persistent directory residual listings.   

Great White Remodeling LLC in Lancaster, New York, exemplifies the secondary market recycling phenomenon. The firm maintains active municipal contractor credentials with the Town of Amherst Building Department and the Town of Tonawanda, as well as an active Better Business Bureau accreditation. Nevertheless, the corporate domain greatwhiteremodeling.com lapsed from active hosting and was listed across aftermarket domain resale exchanges. A comparable pattern appears with Aqua-Care USA in Grimes, Iowa; public federal court records document the entity's commercial activities in distributing and installing walk-in bathtubs and showers, yet the enterprise web asset aquacareusa.com cited in commercial registries is inactive and no longer serves customer traffic.   

Operational Domain Classification	Technical Profile	Representative Entities	Structural Driver
Active Commercial Deployment	Independently maintained root domain with updated service catalogs and direct contact interfaces	O.R.D Inc. Kitchen & Bath Designs (ordinc.com); Connie Moon Design (conniemoondesign.com)	
Centralized brand management and showroom integration

Legacy Subdomain Hosting	Operational website hosted on an Internet Service Provider allocation	Ringbauer Construction & Remodeling Inc. (mobile.ringbauer.comcastbiz.net)	
Low overhead and operational persistence through bundled utility services

Abandoned / Secondary Acquisition	Lapsed registration captured by domain auction platforms while physical operations continue	Great White Remodeling LLC (greatwhiteremodeling.com)	
Divergence between field licensing compliance and web maintenance

Inactive Directory Residuals	Non-resolving domain names preserved solely within aggregator databases	Aqua-Care USA (aquacareusa.com); Tempo Kitchens (tempokitchens.com); Titanium Construction Service (superiorhousetohome.com)	
Historical directory indexing outpacing entity domain renewals

  
In contrast to lapsed properties, functional domains persist where contractors directly integrate digital channels into showroom operations or specialized design consultation models. O.R.D Inc. Kitchen & Bath Designs operates a fully functional web portal at ordinc.com that details local showroom hours, California Contractors State License Board licensing (#893549), and comprehensive design galleries. Similarly, Connie Moon Design in Kent, Washington, operates conniemoondesign.com, serving as a digital showroom for residential cabinetry procurement, finish selections, and spatial design consultations across the Greater Puget Sound. Ringbauer Construction & Remodeling Inc. in Homer Glen, Illinois, presents an enduring alternative model, maintaining continuous operations on a business broadband subdomain (mobile.ringbauer.comcastbiz.net) that serves as an active, functional informational portal.   

Communication Vectors and Inbound Inquiries
Direct communication architectures among independent contractors demonstrate sharp operational bifurcations between free webmail systems, legacy service provider routing, and gated intake mechanisms. Contractors often forgo custom domain email configurations in favor of commercial email providers or Internet service provider mailboxes to reduce administrative overhead.   

O.R.D Inc. utilizes a commercial Google webmail account (ordkitchenbath@gmail.com) published prominently on its primary contact portal, estimate request pages, and social media headers, despite maintaining the operational domain ordinc.com. Ringbauer Construction & Remodeling Inc. utilizes an Internet service provider address (jim@ringbauer.comcastbiz.net) published across its live website and regional directories. Conversely, Connie Moon Design routes prospective clients through a structured, multi-field intake form on conniemoondesign.com/contact/. This structure deliberately obscures raw email addresses from public scraping, ensuring that inquiries capture project scope parameters before initial contractor consultation.

Social Platform Architecture and Local Referral Ecosystems
Social platform deployment varies systematically based on whether a contractor's trade specialty relies primarily on visual aesthetic appeal or utilitarian execution. Surface fabricators and specialized design studios leverage social media as real-time project portfolios, substituting formal standalone websites with dynamic image feeds.   

Pro Elegant Installations in Niles, Michigan, maintains an active Facebook presence to exhibit commercial quartz and granite fabrications, including Calacatta quartz countertops, rather than hosting an independent website. In parallel, Tempo Kitchens in Davie, Florida, established a dedicated Facebook business entity highlighting modern kitchen renovations, custom cabinetry, and local residential installations.   

For utilitarian trade specialties, such as drywall installation, finish carpentry, and general remodeling, formal social media accounts are frequently bypassed in favor of local community referral networks. In South Lyon, Michigan, JHawk Remodeling LLC generates customer intake through homeowner endorsements within localized social discussion groups, where specific references to the firm's craftsmanship and direct telephone contact substitute for public business profiles.   

Analysis of contractor listings also exposes significant classification errors within aggregated public registries. Masterlux LLC in New Port Richey, Florida, which appears within regional contractor directories, operates strictly as an automotive salvage and parts reseller on secondary digital marketplaces rather than a residential kitchen and bathroom remodeling firm. In contrast, firms such as Eastern Cabinet Co in Wilson, North Carolina, demonstrate multi-decade longevity (exceeding 33 continuous years at 3100 Meteor Drive) entirely through physical facility presence, wholesale relationships, and business-to-business subcontracts, maintaining continuous operational viability without active consumer-facing digital channels.

Analysis of Digital Footprint and Communication Infrastructure in Independent Residential Remodeling EnterprisesStructural Divergence Between Trade Viability and First-Party Web InfrastructureAn investigation into independent residential remodeling contractors across diverse United States markets reveals a structural decoupling between commercial longevity and first-party digital visibility. Within the specialized trades of kitchen and bathroom renovations, surface fabrication, and custom cabinetry, enterprises routinely achieve multi-decade operating histories and sustained client volume without maintaining functional proprietary websites or public electronic mail channels. This operational model relies primarily on localized supplier dealer networks, word-of-mouth recommendations, and regional general contracting partnerships rather than direct search engine optimization or inbound digital marketing funnels.The economic mechanisms governing trade contracting explain this digital absence. Because trade contractors operate within defined geographic radii where capacity is constrained by physical labor and skilled craftsmanship, inbound lead volume generated from an open web presence often produces administrative overhead without improving project margins. Instead, contractors integrate directly into material supply ecosystems. Showrooms and trade distributors serve as primary demand aggregators, referring homeowners directly to preferred installation specialists. Consequently, capital and operational resources are allocated toward licensing, physical tools, and supplier credit lines rather than domain hosting, web development, or direct communication infrastructure.Enterprise NameOperating LocationYears in OperationPhysical Showroom or Trade NetworkProprietary Website StatusDirect Enterprise Email AvailabilityTy-Cour Kitchen & BathMadison, IL30+ YearsMarazzi, Berenson HardwareActive (tycour.com)Closed to Public Web InquiriesShoreline Marble & Tile, Inc.Kenosha, WI22 YearsLocal Stone FabricationNone DiscoveredGated Behind TelephonyContaldi Creations, LLCPompton Lakes, NJ5+ YearsFabuwood Dealer NetworkNone DiscoveredGated Behind TelephonyCasa Bella ContractorsHawthorne, NJ20 YearsGAF Certified ContractorAggregator Stub (about.me)Gated Behind TelephonyAbove & Beyond CabinetryHendersonville, NC5+ YearsSemi-Custom Cabinet LinesNone DiscoveredChamber Mailto Webform OnlyIntermediary Platform Dynamics and Direct Contact ObfuscationThe absence of proprietary web infrastructure among small remodeling contractors has accelerated their reliance on third-party digital intermediaries. Platforms such as Houzz, Angi, HomeAdvisor, Birdeye, and the Better Business Bureau function as the default consumer interface for these enterprises. However, these aggregators operate on a lead-generation monetization model that creates significant structural disincentives for publishing direct contact mechanisms.Directory platforms capture consumer intent through algorithmic search results and deliberately route communications through proprietary messaging portals, masked telephone tracking lines, or paid quote-request interfaces. For example, listings on Angi and HomeAdvisor for firms like Pro Crafters in Telford, Pennsylvania, highlight verified client reviews and five-star quality scores while substituting direct corporate email addresses with centralized quote-dispatch workflows. Similarly, the Better Business Bureau records comprehensive operational parameters, corporate start dates, and executive names for firms such as Creative Surfaces & Design LLC in Utah and Ty-Cour Kitchen & Bath in Illinois, but replaces raw email addresses with interactive forms that route through BBB administrative servers.Industrial supplier directories operate under comparable constraints. IndustryNet, which catalogs commercial millwork, stone, and cabinetry suppliers across the Midwest, documents the manufacturing capabilities and physical warehouse location of Ty-Cour Kitchen & Bath in Madison, Illinois, yet explicitly states that the enterprise is not configured to receive inbound electronic mail through open directory channels. Prospective clients must either make direct voice telephone calls or access commercial intermediary services. While these platforms reduce initial overhead, they expose contractors to acute structural vulnerability when hosting providers alter product offerings or retire legacy tools.A clear example of this operational hazard is observed in the life cycle of websites built on Google Business Profiles. For several years, Google allowed contractors to generate free, automated single-page websites hosted under the business.site domain, dynamically populated with reviews, opening hours, and photographs from their Google Business profiles. Following Google’s platform-wide deprecation of all *.business.site domains, these web assets ceased resolving, severing the digital entry point for businesses that had published them across local trade directories.Tile Decor LLC, a specialized tile installation enterprise located in Haledon, New Jersey, illustrates this infrastructural disruption. Despite maintaining an active local reputation with 25 five-star reviews on commercial verification platforms, its published web domain—https://tileinstaller.business.site/—was rendered completely inoperable following Google’s infrastructure decommissioning. Because the business did not establish an independent top-level domain or secondary web host, its digital discoverability collapsed into third-party directory citations.Similar patterns of domain abandonment occur in independent domain registrations where contractors fail to maintain recurring web hosting accounts. Timeless Marble & Granite LLC in Bluffdale, Utah, maintained an active listing associated with the URL fixmygranite.com. However, following periods of consistent local workload and direct supplier sourcing, server hosting for the domain expired, causing the URL to become inactive.
